## Logic 2 (Saleae) Protocol Decoder Management -- Detailed Analysis

### Architecture Overview

Logic 2 uses a **graph-based data flow architecture** where the backend (C++ "graph-io") runs a processing graph of interconnected nodes, and the frontend (TypeScript/React/MobX) creates and manages these nodes via a WebSocket or native module API. Decoders are called "analyzers" and come in two types: **Low-Level Analyzers (LLAs)** -- built-in protocol decoders like SPI, I2C, UART -- and **High-Level Analyzers (HLAs)** -- Python-based extension analyzers that consume LLA output.

---

### 1. Adding Decoders Before vs After Capture

**Key files:**
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\analyzers\AnalyzerManager.tsx` (lines 965-990)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\analyzers\AnalyzersStore.ts` (lines 163-232)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\rapidDataStore\SessionStore.ts` (lines 788-799)

**Adding before capture (no data yet):**

When an analyzer is created via `AnalyzersStore.createAnalyzer()`, it calls `finishEdit()` which applies settings. Inside `finishEdit()` (AnalyzerManager.tsx line 458), the code checks `this.hasCaptureData` before starting analysis. If there is no capture data, analysis is NOT started immediately. Instead, when `startAnalyzerAnalysis()` is called, it enters an **auto-start** mechanism (lines 970-987):

```typescript
if (this.analyzer.areAnalyzerChannelsValid !== 'Valid' || !this.analyzer.hasCaptureData) {
  // setup auto-start.
  this.nodeStartState = 'pending-start';
  this.autoStartDisposer = reaction(
    () => ({
      ready: this.analyzer.areAnalyzerChannelsValid === 'Valid' && this.analyzer.hasCaptureData,
    }),
    ({ ready }) => {
      if (ready) {
        this.cleanupAutoStart();
        this.internalStartAnalysis();
      }
    },
  );
  return;
}
```

This MobX `reaction` watches for when both conditions become true: channels are valid AND capture data exists. Once both are met, `internalStartAnalysis()` fires automatically.

**Adding after capture (data exists):**

When `finishEdit()` is called and `hasCaptureData` is true (line 458-460), it directly calls `this.graphServices.startAnalyzerAnalysis()`, which immediately starts the analysis since data is already available.

**Restriction on adding during recording:**

`SessionStore.canAddAnalyzers` (line 789) restricts adding analyzers to specific capture states. The `addAnalyzerAllowedStates` array does NOT include 'Starting' or 'Recording' for legacy devices, so analyzers cannot be added while actively capturing.

---

### 2. How Decode Range Is Determined

**Key files:**
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\analyzers\AnalyzerManager.tsx` (lines 992-1010)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\timeManager\index.ts` (lines 154-160)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\GraphClient\GraphActions.ts` (lines 82-84)

**Starting time (decode_start):**

The decode starting time is always set to the **beginning of the capture**. In `internalStartAnalysis()` (AnalyzerManager.tsx line 1007):

```typescript
startingTime: this.analyzer.session.timeManager.secondsToGraphTime(0),
```

`secondsToGraphTime(0)` converts 0 seconds (relative to capture start) into an absolute GraphTime by adding it to `_captureStartTime`. This means analysis always starts from the very beginning of the captured data.

**Ending time (decode_end):**

There is **no explicit decode_end** set by the frontend. The backend analyzer node processes all available data from `startingTime` forward until it reaches the end of the captured data stream. The backend reports progress back via the `processedInterval` state, which is a `GraphInterval { begin, end }` in GraphTime coordinates.

**Progress tracking:**

The `percentageProcessed` is computed in `AnalyzerManager` (lines 502-515):

```typescript
get percentageProcessed() {
  if (this.analysisDone) return 1;
  const captureInterval = this.session.processedIntervalSec;
  if (!this.hasProcessedData || captureInterval.end === 0) return 0;
  let percentageProcessed =
    (this.processedInterval.end - captureInterval.begin) / (captureInterval.end - captureInterval.begin);
  percentageProcessed = Math.min(1, percentageProcessed);
  return percentageProcessed;
}
```

This compares the analyzer's `processedInterval.end` against the total capture interval to determine progress percentage.

**For HLAs**, the decode range is determined by the `intervalAvailableForProcessing` reported by the backend, which represents the time range of data available from the connected LLA. The HLA's `percentageProcessed` compares its `processedInterval` against this `availableInterval`.

---

### 3. How Decode Is Triggered After Capture Completes

**Key files:**
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\rapidDataStore\SessionStore.ts` (lines 1077-1078)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\rapidDataStore\RapidDataStore.ts` (line 716)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\analyzers\AnalyzersStore.ts` (lines 234-236)

There are **three distinct paths** that trigger decode:

**Path A: Capture starts with existing analyzers**

In `SessionStore.startCapture()` (line 1077-1078), after the capture transitions to 'Recording' state:

```typescript
this.captureState = 'Recording';
// start all analyzers once the capture has started
this.analyzersStore.startAnalysis();
```

This iterates over all analyzers and calls `startAnalyzerAnalysis()` on each. The analyzers begin processing data in real-time as it arrives from the device.

**Path B: Loading a saved capture file**

In `RapidDataStore.loadSession()` (line 716), after the session is fully loaded and rendering is set up:

```typescript
session.analyzersStore.startAnalysis();
```

This starts all migrated/restored analyzers on the loaded data.

**Path C: Analyzer added or settings changed after capture**

In `AnalyzerManager.finishEdit()` (lines 458-460), when settings are applied and capture data exists:

```typescript
if (this.hasCaptureData) {
  this.graphServices.startAnalyzerAnalysis();
}
```

Additionally, there is a manual "restart" option. The `restartAnalyzerAnalysis()` method (line 959-963) stops and restarts an analyzer, and is available when `session.isPausedOrFinished && areAnalyzerChannelsValid === 'Valid'`.

**Path D: Auto-start reaction**

If an analyzer was added before data existed, the auto-start MobX reaction (described above) fires when data becomes available, calling `internalStartAnalysis()`.

---

### 4. Decoder Lifecycle Management

**Key files:**
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\analyzers\AnalyzersStore.ts` (lines 163-232)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\analyzers\AnalyzerManager.tsx` (lines 826-1171, the `GraphServices` inner class)
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\GraphClient\GraphClient.ts` (lines 499-568, 1082-1115)

**CREATE phase:**

1. `AnalyzersStore.createAnalyzer()` calls `backend.operations.createAnalyzer()` which sends graph actions to:
   - Add an `AnalyzerNode` to the graph
   - Add pipes for render requests/results
   - Start node execution
   - Load analyzer plugins from paths
   - Select the specific analyzer by name
   - Open a database for storing results

2. An `AnalyzerManager` instance is created with the returned `nodeId`, `portId`, default settings, and database path.

3. `finishEdit()` is called to apply settings, which calls `graphServices.updateSettings()`:
   - Stops any existing analysis
   - Clears old results
   - Sends new settings to the backend via `updateAnalyzerSettings`
   - Sets up subscriptions for progress, bubble/marker channels, and database generation
   - If capture data exists, starts analysis

**START DECODE phase:**

`GraphServices.internalStartAnalysis()` (lines 992-1010):
1. Prepares channel mappings -- maps each channel setting to its data store node ID
2. Sends `startAnalyzer` operation to the backend, which:
   - Connects each channel's data store node to the analyzer node via `connectApi` (from storeNodeId to analyzer nodeId at toApiIndex=llaChannelNumber)
   - Sends `legacyAnalyzerActions.startAnalysis({ startingTime })` to the analyzer node

**GET RESULTS phase:**

Results are received through **state subscriptions** set up in `GraphServices.newAnalyzerSubscriptions()` (lines 1025-1171):

1. **Progress subscription**: Receives `processedInterval`, `framesAnalyzed`, `framesReleased`, `analysisDone`, `isAnalyzing`, `errorState`, `gapsExistInAnalyzedData`. Updates the `processedInterval` on the analyzer manager and tracks progress.

2. **Channels with bubbles/markers subscription**: Receives which channels have decode bubbles and markers, used for UI rendering.

3. **Database generation subscription**: Receives `databaseGeneration`, `completedDatabaseGeneration`, `databaseOutOfSpace`, `framesWritten`, `dataColumnsSeen`. Used for the FrameDB query system that powers the data table.

The `analysisDone` boolean signals that the analyzer has finished processing all available data.

**DELETE phase:**

`AnalyzerManager.delete()` (lines 545-603) follows a structured teardown:
1. `teardownMobx()` -- removes MobX reactions and auto-start
2. `teardownDisconnectFromBackend()` -- disconnects terminal pipe and query node
3. `teardownGraphNodes()` -- deletes the analyzer node from the backend graph, disconnects any HLAs connected to this LLA
4. `teardownCleanupFrontendManagedResources()` -- deletes the database directory
5. `teardownCleanupDatabaseOnShutdown()` -- removes the shutdown cleanup callback

---

### Summary of Key Design Patterns

| Aspect | Logic 2 Approach |
|--------|-----------------|
| **Decoder before capture** | Auto-start reaction watches for data availability; starts when both channels valid and data exists |
| **Decoder after capture** | Immediately starts analysis in `finishEdit()` if `hasCaptureData` is true |
| **Decode range** | Always starts from time 0 (capture start); no explicit end -- backend processes all available data |
| **Post-capture decode trigger** | `analyzersStore.startAnalysis()` called after capture starts, after file load, or via auto-start reaction |
| **Lifecycle** | Create (graph node + load plugin + open DB) -> Start (connect data stores + startAnalysis) -> Results (state subscriptions) -> Delete (teardown graph + cleanup DB) |
| **Architecture** | Graph-based: data flows from DeviceNode -> Disaggregator -> StoreNodes -> AnalyzerNode via API connections |
| **Progress tracking** | Backend reports `processedInterval` and `analysisDone` via state subscriptions; frontend computes percentage |
| **Data persistence** | Analyzer results stored in FrameDB (SQLite); QueryNode provides indexed access for data table |

现在让我对比两个方案的核心差异。

## Logic 2 vs PXView 解码器管理方案对比

### Logic 2 的方案（简洁）

Logic 2 用的是**声明式 + 响应式**架构：

1. **不设 decode_end** — 解码器永远从时间0开始，后端自动处理到数据末尾。没有 `decode_start`/`decode_end` 的手动管理。
2. **MobX reaction 自动启动** — 添加解码器时如果没有数据，设一个 `reaction` 监听器，等数据就绪自动启动：
   ```typescript
   reaction(() => ({
     ready: this.analyzer.areAnalyzerChannelsValid === 'Valid' && this.analyzer.hasCaptureData,
   }), ({ ready }) => {
     if (ready) this.internalStartAnalysis();
   });
   ```
3. **图计算架构** — 数据从 DeviceNode → StoreNode → AnalyzerNode 自动流动，不需要手动调用 `add_decode_task`、`frame_ended()`、`set_capture_end_flag()` 这些步骤。

### PXView 当前的问题

PXView 需要手动管理一堆状态：

| 手动管理的状态 | 作用 | 问题 |
|---|---|---|
| `set_capture_end_flag(true)` | 标记采集结束 | 容易忘记调用 |
| `frame_ended()` | 通知解码帧结束 | 和 capture_end_flag 重复 |
| `set_options_changed(true)` | 标记选项已变更 | 又一个手动标志 |
| `add_decode_task(de)` | 手动提交解码任务 | 需要在正确时机调用 |
| `set_decode_region()` | 设置解码范围 | `_decode_end=0` 导致只解码0个样本 |
| `_empty_decode_traces` | 采集前暂存解码器 | 需要在 `set_active_document` 时手动转移 |
| `DSV_MSG_COPY_TO_DOC_DONE` | 采集完成后触发解码 | 需要手动遍历所有 decode_traces |

**根本原因**：PXView 的解码管线是**命令式**的，每一步都需要调用者手动编排正确的调用顺序。而 Logic 2 是**声明式**的，只需声明"我要解码这个通道"，框架自动处理其余。

### 建议的改进方向

最直接的改进：**让 `execute_decode_stack()` 在 `decode_end == 0` 时自动使用完整数据范围**（已做），并且**让 `DSV_MSG_COPY_TO_DOC_DONE` 成为唯一的解码触发点**，而不是在 `add_decoder` 里也手动触发。

当前代码已经基本实现了这个思路：
- 采集前添加解码器 → 只设 `options_changed`，等 `COPY_TO_DOC_DONE` 自动启动
- 采集后添加解码器 → 设 `capture_end_flag` + `frame_ended()` + 延迟 `add_decode_task`

但比 Logic 2 多了 `capture_end_flag` 和 `frame_ended()` 这两个手动步骤。Logic 2 的后端根本不需要这些——它只看"有没有数据"，有就处理到末尾。

**如果要做更彻底的简化**，可以让 `DecoderStack::execute_decode_stack()` 自己判断：
- 如果 `_snapshot` 存在且非空，自动从0解码到 `_sample_count-1`
- 不需要外部设置 `capture_end_flag`、`frame_ended()`、`decode_end`

这样就消除了所有手动状态管理，和 Logic 2 的方案一致。但这是较大的重构，需要评估对现有手动操作流程的影响。

**Assistant**

**没必要做这个重构。** 理由：

1. **当前方案已经工作** — MCP先加解码器再采集，测试通过，覆盖完整500M样本。

2. **风险大于收益** — `capture_end_flag`、`frame_ended()`、`options_changed` 这些状态在手动操作流程（GUI点击添加解码器）中也被使用。重构 `execute_decode_stack()` 的自动判断逻辑会影响所有解码路径，包括实时刷新模式（`is_realtime_refresh`），那里 `decode_end` 的行为不同（用 `max` 而非 `min`）。

3. **Logic 2 的方案依赖完全不同的架构** — 它是图计算 + MobX响应式，数据自动流动。PXView 是命令式架构，信号/槽驱动。把声明式的思路硬塞进命令式框架，反而增加复杂度。

4. **MCP场景的特殊性** — MCP只需要"先加解码器再采集"这一条规则，`DSV_MSG_COPY_TO_DOC_DONE` 自动启动解码已经够用。不需要为MCP单独重构整个解码管线。

**结论**：当前修复已经解决了"解码只覆盖前半段"的问题，保持现状即可。

两个问题：

### 1. `create_session` 报错原因

测试脚本里 `call_tool("create_session")` 调用的是 MCP tools 接口，但 `create_session` 是 JSON-RPC 方法，不是 MCP tool。MCP tool 列表里没有 `create_session`。

实际上**不需要手动创建session** — `add_analyzer` 已经自动创建了（上次修改加的逻辑）。测试脚本里的 `create_session` 调用可以删掉。

### 2. PXView 多标签页 vs Logic 2 的区别

| | PXView | Logic 2 |
|---|---|---|
| **标签页** | 多标签，每个 `TabContext` 绑定独立的 View + SigSession + Document | 单窗口单会话，没有多标签 |
| **Session** | `SigSession` 是核心单例，但每个 TabContext 有自己的数据 | `SessionStore` 是唯一会话，所有数据共享 |
| **设备** | 同一时刻只能一个标签页使用设备采集 | 同理，同一时刻只有一个采集 |
| **MCP session** | `AppService` 管理 `SessionService` 映射，但底层都是同一个 `SigSession` | 不需要映射，直接操作唯一会话 |

**PXView 需要显式创建 session 的原因**：`AppService` 维护了一个 `_sessions` map，`get_active_session()` 返回 `SessionService*`。MCP 调用需要先有 `SessionService` 才能操作。但底层实际上只有一个 `SigSession`，所以这个映射层是多余的复杂度。

**实际效果**：`add_analyzer` 和 `start_capture` 都已经加了自动创建 session 的逻辑，所以 MCP 客户端不需要手动调用 `create_session`。这个方法只存在于 JSON-RPC 层，不在 MCP tool 列表里。

## ATK-Logic Architecture Analysis

### 1. Tab System

**Yes, atk-logic implements a full multi-tab system.** Multiple captures can be open simultaneously, each in its own tab.

**Key files:**
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\qml\control\ATabView.qml` -- The tab container
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\qml\control\ATabButton.qml` -- Individual tab button rendering
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\qml\session\Session.qml` -- The content area per tab

**How it works:**

`ATabView` uses a `StackLayout` (QML) to switch between session content areas. Each tab is backed by a `Session` QML component dynamically created via `Qt.createQmlObject()`. The tab buttons are rendered in a horizontal `ListView` at the bottom of the window (in a status bar area), using a `ListModel` called `tabButtonModle`.

The `createSession()` function in `ATabView.qml` (line 491-535) is the core tab creation logic:
- It dynamically creates a `Session{}` QML object and adds it to the `StackLayout`
- It appends an entry to `tabButtonModle` (the ListModel driving tab buttons)
- Device sessions are inserted at position 1 (after the Home tab); file sessions are appended at the end
- Tab selection switches the `StackLayout.currentIndex`

Each tab button shows:
- A green connection indicator dot (when `isConnect===1`)
- The session name
- A close button (only for `SessionType.File` tabs)
- A progress animation bar during capture

Right-click context menu on tabs supports: Save, Close, Close All, Close Saved, Close Right, Close Others.

The `Home` tab (first tab, `SessionType.Demo`) is always present and shows a "Please connect device" screen with Demo and Open File buttons.

---

### 2. Multiple Devices

**No, atk-logic does NOT support multiple devices simultaneously.** Only one physical device can be connected at a time.

**Evidence:**

- `FramelessWindow::m_connectPort` (line 196 of `frameless_window.h`) is a single `qint32`, not a list. It tracks the one connected device port.

- `FramelessWindow::onDevicePlugIN()` (line 522-549 of `frameless_window.cpp`) checks `if(m_connectPort==-1)` before attempting to connect a new device. If a device is already connected (`m_connectPort != -1`), it ignores new device arrivals.

- `USBServer` does have internal data structures that could theoretically hold multiple devices (`QMap<int, USBControl*> m_usbControl`), but the application-level logic in `FramelessWindow` only ever tracks one `m_connectPort`.

- When a device is unplugged, `onDevicePlugOUT()` calls `usbService->DeviceToFile()` which converts the device session into a file session (disconnecting the USB binding), and resets `m_connectPort=-1`.

- The `DataService` singleton holds a `QHash<QString, Session*> m_session_list` which can hold multiple sessions, but only one of them is ever a live device session at a time.

**Device lifecycle:**
1. Device plugs in -> `onDevicePlugIN()` -> `USBServer::GetNewDevice()` -> `ConnectDevice::CheckDeviceCreanInfo()` (async thread) -> `receiveDeviceCreanInfo()` -> `createSession()` with `SessionType.Device`
2. Device unplugs -> `onDevicePlugOUT()` -> `USBServer::DeviceToFile()` -> emits `deviceToFile()` signal -> QML converts the tab from Device type to File type (`isConnect=2`), unbinding the USB port
3. Device re-plugs -> if a matching disconnected session exists (same name, level, channel count, `isConnect===2`), it reconnects to that existing tab via `Signal.resetConnectDevice()` rather than creating a new tab

---

### 3. Session Management

**There is one Session per tab, managed by a global DataService singleton.**

**Key files:**
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\pv\static\data_service.h` / `.cpp` -- Global session registry
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\pv\data\session.h` / `.cpp` -- Per-session data model
- `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\pv\controller\session_controller.h` / `.cpp` -- Per-session QML-facing controller

**Architecture:**

```
DataService (singleton)
  |-- m_session_list: QHash<QString, Session*>     // All sessions by random 16-char ID
  |-- m_sessionError_list: QHash<QString, SessionError*>
  |-- USBServer (singleton)                         // USB device registry
  |-- DecodeService (singleton)                     // Protocol decoder engine
```

Each `Session` object owns:
- `Segment* m_segment` -- The raw captured data (multi-channel, compressed block storage)
- `Segment* m_segmentRecode` -- Original data before glitch filtering
- `SessionConfig* m_config` -- Display/zoom configuration
- `ThreadWork* m_workThread` -- Background worker for capture, save, load, glitch removal
- `QHash<QString, DecodeController*> m_decodeList` -- Protocol decoders for this session
- `QVector<MeasureData> m_measure` -- Measurement annotations
- `QVector<VernierData> m_vernier` -- Cursor/vernier annotations

Each `SessionController` is a QML-accessible wrapper that:
- Is instantiated as a QML component inside each `Session.qml`
- Bridges QML properties (`sessionName`, `sessionType`, `sessionPort`, `channelCount`, `filePath`) to the C++ `Session`
- Auto-initializes when all required properties are set (via `cisInit()` check)
- Calls `DataService::newSession()` to create the backend `Session`
- Binds the USB port to the session via `USBServer::BindingPort()`

**Session types** (enum `SessionType`):
- `Demo=0` -- The Home tab, no real data
- `Device=1` -- Live capture from USB device
- `File=2` -- Opened from a `.atkdl` file

---

### 4. Capture-Data-UI Relationship

**The data flow follows this path:**

```
USB Device
  |
  v
USBControl (libusb read loop in ThreadRead)
  |
  v
ThreadWork::DeviceRecvThread()  [background thread]
  |-- Fills Segment with raw samples
  |-- Emits timerDrawUpdate() for periodic UI refresh
  |-- Emits SendDeviceRecvSchedule() for progress bar
  |-- Emits appendMeasure/appendVernier for auto-detection
  |
  v
SessionController (signal relay)
  |-- sessionDrawUpdate -> QML sSignal.channelRefresh()
  |-- sendDeviceRecvSchedule -> QML progressBar updates
  |
  v
QML Session Component
  |-- DrawBackground (C++ QQuickPaintedItem) renders waveform
  |-- DrawBackgroundFloor handles mouse interaction
  |-- ChannelsView contains the channel rows
  |-- SideBar + SideBarContent for settings/decode/measure panels
```

**Rendering architecture:**
- `DrawBackground` and `DrawBackgroundFloor` are C++ `QQuickPaintedItem` subclasses registered as QML types
- They receive a `sessionID` and look up the `Session`/`Segment` from `DataService` to paint
- Double buffering is implemented (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\atk-logic-master\pv\draw\double_buffering.h`)
- The `Segment` class uses a two-level compressed storage: `_Node` structures with `tog`/`value` for run-length encoding of constant regions, plus `lbp` pointers to 64-byte sub-blocks for actual data

**Capture modes:**
- Single capture (non-buffer mode): captures until trigger + depth, then stops
- Buffer mode: circular buffer capture
- Loop mode: repeated capture with configurable hold percentage (controlled by `sConfig.loopState` in QML)

---

### 5. Overall Architecture Comparison

**ATK-Logic is architecturally much simpler than PXView, and is NOT similar to Saleae Logic 2.**

**Comparison with PXView:**

| Aspect | PXView | ATK-Logic |
|--------|--------|-----------|
| UI Framework | Qt6 C++ Widgets | Qt QML + C++ backend |
| Tab System | `DraggableTabWidget` with drag-out-to-new-window | Simple `StackLayout` with bottom tab bar, no drag-out |
| Multi-device | Yes, multiple devices simultaneously | No, single device only |
| Session per tab | `TabContext` binds View+Session+Document | `Session` QML component with `SessionController` |
| Session states | LIVE / HISTORICAL | Device / File / Demo (implicit) |
| Data storage | `SessionDocument` + `SessionSnapshot` + disk cache | `Segment` (in-memory compressed blocks) |
| Disk cache | Full tiered memory+disk system | None; all data in RAM |
| Protocol decoders | 215 C decoders (DLL) + 220+ Python decoders | Python decoders only (via `atk_decoder_*` API) |
| Callback system | Interface-based (`ISessionCallback`, `IMessageListener`, etc.) | Qt signals/slots + QML signal/slot |
| Window system | `MainFrame` (borderless) + `SubMainFrame` (detached) | `FramelessWindow` (single window only) |
| Device abstraction | `DeviceAgent` wrapping `libsigrok` | Direct `USBControl` wrapping `libusb` |
| Hardware drivers | `libsigrok` with multiple driver backends | Direct USB protocol (ATK-Logic specific) |

**Comparison with Saleae Logic 2:**

ATK-Logic is also quite different from Saleae Logic 2:
- Saleae supports multiple simultaneous devices; ATK-Logic does not
- Saleae has an analyzers-per-capture model; ATK-Logic has decoders-per-session
- Saleae uses a document-centric model with separate capture/analysis; ATK-Logic mixes capture and analysis in one session
- Saleae's UI is custom C++ with hardware acceleration; ATK-Logic uses QML with software rendering (`QSGRendererInterface::Software`)

**ATK-Logic's architecture is best described as:**
- A **QML-fronted, C++-backed** single-window application
- With a **tab-per-session** model (one tab = one capture/file)
- **Single device only** -- the device is exclusive to one tab at a time
- **Session = Tab = Capture unit** -- each tab has its own `Session` object with its own `Segment` (data), `ThreadWork` (worker), `DecodeController` list, and display configuration
- **Global singletons** for USB management (`USBServer`), data service (`DataService`), and decode service (`DecodeService`)
- **Device disconnect converts a live session to a file session** in-place, preserving the tab and data

## Logic 2 (Saleae) Architecture Analysis: Multi-Device, Multi-Session, and Panel/Tab System

### 1. Does Logic 2 Support Multiple Devices Simultaneously? How?

**Yes, Logic 2 supports multiple devices simultaneously, but only one device is active at a time.**

The `DeviceListManager` (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\rapidDataStore\DeviceListManager.ts`) tracks all connected devices:

- **`readyConnectedDevices`** (line 74): An observable array of all devices in the "DSReady" state.
- **`allConnectedDevices`** (line 77): An observable array of all connected devices including those initializing or in error states.
- **`physicalDevices`** (computed, line 135): Filters for non-simulation physical devices.
- **`hasPhysicalDevice`** (computed, line 146): Returns true if any physical device is connected.

When a new device connects, `handleNewDevices()` (line 186) automatically creates a new session for it:

```typescript
await this.rapidDataStore.createSession({
  type: 'newDevice',
  newDevice: nextDeviceState as ConnectedLegacyDeviceV2 | ConnectedDeviceV2WithMSO,
});
```

However, the **device acquisition model** is exclusive. The abstract `DeviceManager` class (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\device\DeviceManager.ts`, lines 9-18) documents this clearly:

> "Because multiple sessions can share the same device, we use the concept of acquisition to indicate which session has exclusive access to the device. Only the active session can acquire a device. When a different session becomes active, or when we transition to no active session, the previously active session will release the device."

Key points:
- Multiple devices can be connected simultaneously (tracked by `DeviceListManager`)
- Each device gets its own session/tab when connected
- Only the **active session** acquires its device -- when you switch tabs, the old session releases its device and the new session acquires its device
- The `DevicesMenuControlled` component (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\application\compositions\DeviceManagement\DevicesMenuControlled.tsx`) shows a button displaying the count of connected physical devices (e.g., "2 Devices")

### 2. What Is the "Splitter" or "Panel" Architecture? Can You Have Multiple Captures in the Same Window?

**There is no splitter or multi-panel architecture for showing multiple captures simultaneously.** Logic 2 uses a **single-session-at-a-time display model**.

The main window layout (from `App.tsx` at `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\application\App.tsx`) is:

```
+------------------------------------------+
|              TopArea / Toolbar            |
+------+---------------------------+-------+
|      |                           |       |
| Chan |    GraphArea (single      | Side  |
| Label|    active session only)   | bar   |
|      |                           |       |
+------+---------------------------+-------+
|           BottomBar (Tabs + Controls)    |
+------------------------------------------+
```

The `GraphArea` component (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\application\compositions\GraphRows\index.tsx`, lines 386-403) shows either:
- A `ConnectedSessionPlaceholder` (when no session is active)
- A single `GraphAreaWithSession` (the active session's waveform display)

There is **no mechanism to show two sessions side-by-side** or split the view. The `ActiveSessionMounter` component (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\utils\ActiveSessionMounter.tsx`) conditionally renders its children only when `rapidDataStore.hasActiveSession` is true -- it renders exactly one session's content.

The `Sidebar` (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\sidebar\index.tsx`) is a right-side panel with tabs for: devices, analyzers, cursors, extensions, measurements, notes, and timing markers. It is a single panel with tab switching, not a multi-panel layout.

### 3. How Does SessionStore Handle Multiple Captures or Multiple Devices?

The `RapidDataStore` (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\rapidDataStore\RapidDataStore.ts`) is the central orchestrator. It manages:

**Session Collection** (line 130-131):
```typescript
private _sessions: ObservableMap<number, SessionStore> = observable.map(undefined, { deep: false });
```

**Active Session** (line 137):
```typescript
@observable.ref private _activeSession?: SessionStore;
```

**Session Creation Types** (lines 87-99):
```typescript
export type CreateSessionProps =
  | { type: 'loadFile'; persistedData: PersistedData; loadSessionStatus?: LoadSessionStatus }
  | { type: 'newDevice'; newDevice: DeviceState.KnownConnectedDevice; sourceSession?: SessionStore }
  | { type: 'reset'; sourceSession: SessionStore }
  | { type: 'newTab'; sourceSession?: SessionStore }
  | { type: 'automationCapture'; device: DeviceState.KnownConnectedDevice };
```

Each `SessionStore` (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\rapidDataStore\SessionStore.ts`) is bound to exactly **one device** through its `DeviceManager` (line 199). The `SessionStore` contains:
- Its own `DeviceManager` (either `SessionDeviceManager` for legacy Logic devices, or `MSODeviceManager` for MSO devices)
- Its own `RowsManager`, `RenderingManager`, `ChannelsStore`, `AnalyzersStore`, `TimeManager`, etc.
- Its own capture state machine (`CaptureState`: Empty, Starting, Recording, Stopping, Finished, Deleting, etc.)

**Session switching** is serialized and guarded (lines 626-651):
- `isSwitchingSessionsAllowed` prevents switching during active recording
- `isCreatingSession` prevents concurrent session creation
- When switching sessions, the old session's device is released and the new session's device is acquired

**When you start a new capture on a session that already has data**, the system creates a brand new session (via `resetSession`, line 724-730) and deletes the old one:

```typescript
async resetSession() {
    const session = this.activeSession;
    const newSession = await this.createSession({ type: 'reset', sourceSession: session });
    await session.delete({ removeSessionFromState: false });
    return newSession;
}
```

### 4. Is There a Tab System or Is It a Single-Session Design?

**Logic 2 has a full tab system.** Multiple sessions can exist simultaneously, each represented as a tab.

The tab system is implemented in `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\tabs\index.tsx`:

- **`SessionTab`** component (lines 27-65): Renders each session as a tab with:
  - Editable name (`session.name`)
  - Active state highlighting
  - Close button (with protection logic)
  - Save-before-close option
  - Click handler to switch active session

- **`Tabs`** component (lines 67-186): Renders the scrollable tab bar with:
  - All sessions from `rapidDataStore.displayOrderedSessions`
  - A "+" button to create new sessions
  - Smooth scroll animation to newly created tabs

**Tab commands** (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\tabs\commands.ts`):
```typescript
export const Commands = {
  closeSession: 'tabs:close-session',
  createSession: 'tabs:create-session',
  nextSession: 'tabs:next-session',
  previousSession: 'tabs:previous-session',
  closeOtherSessions: 'tab:close-all-others',
};
```

**Tab events** (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\tabs\events.tsx`):
- `createSession`: Creates a new tab using the active session's device
- `nextSession` / `previousSession`: Navigate between tabs
- `closeSession`: Close the current or specified tab
- `closeOtherSessions`: Close all tabs except the active one

**Key constraint**: Only one session is **displayed** at a time. When you click a tab, `rapidDataStore.setActiveSession(session)` is called, which:
1. Calls `_onInactive()` on the old session (releases its device)
2. Calls `_onActive()` on the new session (acquires its device)
3. Updates `_activeSession`

### 5. How Does the Window Layout Work?

The window layout is a **single-window, single-session-display** design:

**App.tsx** (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\application\App.tsx`, lines 77-89):
```
<div className={styles.appContent}>
  <div className={styles.mainArea}>
    <GraphArea />          -- The waveform display (or session placeholder)
    <ExtensionDetail />    -- Extension detail panel
    <Sidebar />            -- Right sidebar with panel tabs
  </div>
  <BottomBar />            -- Bottom bar with device menu, tabs, and controls
</div>
```

**BottomBar** (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\application\compositions\BottomBar.tsx`):
- `ConnectedDeviceMenu` -- Shows connected device count and device selection
- `Tabs` -- The tab bar for switching between sessions
- `AutomationController` -- Automation button
- `ZoomController` or MSO-specific controls (layout menu, time range menu)

**GraphArea** (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\application\compositions\GraphRows\index.tsx`):
- When no session: Shows `ConnectedSessionPlaceholder` (prompts to connect device or load file)
- When session active: Shows `GraphAreaWithSession` which renders:
  - `TopArea` -- Timing display, channel label header
  - Scrollable `RowsInternal` -- The actual channel rows (digital, analog, analyzer)
  - `LabelResizer` -- Draggable divider between channel labels and graph
  - `OverlayData` -- Overlay elements (cursors, markers)
  - `StripChartScrollBar` -- Horizontal scrollbar for MSO waveform mode

**Sidebar** (`C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\sidebar\index.tsx`):
- Resizable right-side panel
- Panel switcher icons for: devices, analyzers, cursors, extensions, measurements, notes, timing markers
- Only one panel content shown at a time

### Summary Table

| Question | Answer |
|----------|--------|
| Multiple devices simultaneously? | Yes, tracked by `DeviceListManager`. Each gets its own session/tab. But only one device is "acquired" (active) at a time. |
| Splitter/panel for multiple captures? | No. Single-session display only. No side-by-side or split-view. |
| SessionStore multi-capture handling? | Each `SessionStore` = one device + one capture. New capture on existing data creates a fresh session (reset). Multiple sessions exist in `RapidDataStore._sessions` map. |
| Tab system? | Yes. Full tab bar at bottom. Each tab = one `SessionStore`. Keyboard shortcuts for next/previous/close. |
| Window layout? | Single window: TopArea + GraphArea + Sidebar on right + BottomBar (with tabs). Only the active session's data is rendered. |
| Workspace concept? | No workspace concept exists. No "splitter" or "multi-panel" architecture. |

## 三个逻辑分析仪的多标签/多设备架构对比

### 核心对比

| | **PXView** | **Logic 2 (Saleae)** | **ATK-Logic** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **多设备同时连接** | 支持 | 支持（但同一时刻只激活一个） | 不支持，单设备 |
| **多标签** | 支持，可拖出独立窗口 | 支持，底部标签栏 | 支持，底部标签栏 |
| **标签=会话** | `TabContext` 绑定 View+Session+Document | `SessionStore` = 一个标签 | `Session` QML组件 = 一个标签 |
| **设备获取模型** | 设备绑定到当前活动标签 | acquire/release：切换标签时释放旧设备、获取新设备 | 单设备独占，拔出后转为文件会话 |
| **同时显示多个会话** | 支持（拖出标签到独立窗口） | 不支持，同一时刻只渲染一个会话 | 不支持，StackLayout切换 |
| **UI框架** | Qt6 C++ Widgets | React + TypeScript + C++后端 | QML + C++后端 |

### 关键架构差异

**1. Logic 2 的"分栏"**

Logic 2 底部有标签栏 + 设备选择菜单，但**不是分栏显示**。它的架构是：
- 多个设备可以同时连接（`DeviceListManager` 跟踪所有设备）
- 每个设备自动创建一个 `SessionStore`（标签）
- **同一时刻只有一个标签是"acquired"状态** — 切换标签时旧标签释放设备、新标签获取设备
- 图表区域只渲染当前活动会话

所以 Logic 2 是**多设备多会话，但单会话显示**。

**2. ATK-Logic 的简单模型**

- 全局只有一个 `m_connectPort`，设备插入时如果已有设备就忽略
- 设备拔出时，设备会话原地转为文件会话（`DeviceToFile`），标签保留
- 没有 acquire/release 概念，因为根本不可能同时连两个设备

**3. PXView 的独特之处**

PXView 是三者中唯一支持**同时显示多个会话**的（拖出标签到独立窗口）。但底层 `SigSession` 实际上是共享的，`TabContext` 只是一个绑定层。

### 对 MCP 的影响

当前 PXView 的 MCP 层有 `AppService` → `SessionService` 的映射，但底层只有一个 `SigSession`。这和 Logic 2 的 `RapidDataStore._sessions: ObservableMap<number, SessionStore>` 不同 — Logic 2 每个标签真的有独立的 `SessionStore`（独立设备、独立数据、独立解码器）。

PXView 的多标签更像是**视图层的多标签**（同一个数据源的不同视图），而不是**数据层的多会话**。所以 MCP 的 `create_session` 映射层其实是多余的 — 它总是返回同一个 `SigSession` 的包装。

你说得对，我之前的分析不够准确。让我重新理解PXView的数据层架构。

现在我理解了。你说得对，PXView 的架构确实是数据层和硬件层分离的：

## PXView 的真实架构

```
硬件层 (SigSession)                    数据层 (SessionDocument)
┌─────────────────────┐               ┌──────────────────────┐
│ _capture_data       │──copy──→      │ Document A (Tab 1)   │
│ (采集缓冲区, 实时)    │               │  ├─ _logic (Snapshot) │
│                     │               │  ├─ _analog           │
│ _view_data ─────────┼──→ 信号显示    │  ├─ _dso             │
│ (当前视图数据)        │               │  └─ _decode_traces   │
└─────────────────────┘               ├──────────────────────┤
                                      │ Document B (Tab 2)   │
  SigSession 只有一个                   │  └─ 独立的Snapshot    │
  但 SessionDocument 可以有多个          └──────────────────────┘
```

**关键设计**：
- **采集时**：`SigSession` 持有 `_capture_data`（硬件缓冲区），信号直接从硬件读取
- **采集结束**：`copy_data_to_document()` 把数据深拷贝到 `SessionDocument` 的 Snapshot 中
- **每个 TabContext** 有自己的 `SessionDocument`，拥有独立的 Snapshot 副本
- **切换标签**：`set_active_document()` 切换 `_active_document`，不同标签的数据互不干扰
- **设备断开**：Document 里的 Snapshot 仍然保留，可以继续查看和解码

这和 Logic 2 的区别：

| | **PXView** | **Logic 2** |
|---|---|---|
| 数据所有权 | `SessionDocument` 拥有独立 Snapshot 副本 | `SessionStore` 拥有数据，图计算节点引用 |
| 数据复制 | `copy_data_to_document` 深拷贝 | 不复制，数据在图节点间流动 |
| 多标签数据 | 每个标签有独立副本，可独立解码 | 每个标签有独立 SessionStore，数据不共享 |
| 设备断开后 | Document 保留数据，标签可继续操作 | 同理，Session 保留数据 |

所以 PXView 的多标签是**真正的数据层多会话** — 每个 `SessionDocument` 有独立的 Snapshot、独立的解码器列表。只是 `SigSession`（硬件控制层）是单例，因为同一时刻只有一个设备在采集。

这意味着 MCP 的 `SessionService` 映射确实有存在的意义 — 不同的 session 可以对应不同的 `SessionDocument`（标签），只是当前实现把它们都映射到了同一个 `SigSession` + 同一个 `_active_document`。如果要支持 MCP 操作不同标签的数据，需要让 `SessionService` 能切换 `_active_document`。

**User**

所以他们两个在效果上有什么区别

**Assistant**

**效果上几乎没区别**，用户体验是一样的：

| 用户操作 | PXView | Logic 2 |
|---|---|---|
| 打开多个标签 | 各标签有独立数据 | 各标签有独立数据 |
| 切换标签 | 切换 `_active_document` | 切换 `_activeSession`，acquire/release 设备 |
| 在标签上解码 | 各标签独立解码 | 各标签独立解码 |
| 重新采集 | 覆盖当前标签数据 | 创建全新 Session（旧数据丢弃） |
| 关闭标签 | 删除 Document | 删除 Session |

唯一的实际差异：

1. **PXView 可以拖出标签到独立窗口**，同时看两个标签的数据。Logic 2 同一时刻只能看一个标签。

2. **内存模型不同**：PXView 每次采集结束做 `copy_data_to_document` 深拷贝，每个标签占独立内存。Logic 2 的数据在图节点间流动，不需要深拷贝。所以 PXView 多标签更吃内存，但数据完全独立互不影响。

3. **设备切换**：Logic 2 有显式的 acquire/release（切换标签时旧标签释放设备）。PXView 切换标签只是换 `_active_document`，设备始终被 SigSession 持有。

**总结**：对用户来说体验基本一致。底层实现差异（深拷贝 vs 图流动、单 SigSession vs 多 SessionStore）是工程选择，不影响功能。

## Saleae Logic 2 Automation & MCP API -- Complete Reference

Logic 2 exposes **two separate remote control interfaces** from its source code:

1. **gRPC Automation API** (legacy, Python `saleae` package talks to this)
2. **MCP (Model Context Protocol) Server** (new, for AI assistants like Claude)

Both are implemented in the same application and expose functionally equivalent operations.

---

### 1. MCP Server (Model Context Protocol)

**Source directory:** `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\mcp\`

**Key files:**
- `McpServer.ts` -- Main server class, default port **10530**, host `127.0.0.1`
- `protocol.ts` -- JSON-RPC 2.0 protocol handler, MCP protocol version `2025-03-26`
- `transport/http.ts` -- HTTP transport (POST-based JSON-RPC)
- `defineTool.ts` -- Tool definition helper with type-safe schema binding
- `tools.ts` -- Tool registry (all 15 tools)
- `handlers/` -- Individual tool handler implementations
- `types.ts` -- Type definitions (McpError, HandlerContext, ToolResult, etc.)

**Transport:** HTTP POST with JSON-RPC 2.0 body. CORS enabled (`Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`). Request timeout disabled (to support long-running `wait_capture` calls). Schema validation via AJV with `coerceTypes: true`.

**MCP Protocol Methods Supported:**
- `initialize` -- Returns server info (`saleae-logic2`), protocol version, capabilities
- `notifications/initialized` -- Client acknowledgment (no response)
- `ping` -- Health check
- `tools/list` -- Lists all available tools with schemas
- `tools/call` -- Invokes a tool by name with arguments

**Docs URL:** `https://docs.saleae.com/mcp`

**MCP Error Codes:**
| Code | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| `INVALID_REQUEST` | Bad parameters or invalid state |
| `MISSING_DEVICE` | No device connected or found |
| `DEVICE_ERROR` | Device communication error |
| `INTERNAL_ERROR` | Unexpected internal error |
| `EXPORT_FAILED` | Export operation failed |
| `LOAD_CAPTURE_FAILED` | Could not load capture file |
| `OUT_OF_MEMORY` | Capture stopped due to memory exhaustion |

---

### 2. gRPC Automation API

**Source directory:** `C:\Users\admin\Downloads\Logic_source_new\original_source\app\services\automation\`

**Key files:**
- `server.ts` -- `AutomationServer` class, default port **10430**, host `127.0.0.1`
- `validation.ts` -- `AutomationError` class with `ErrorCode` enum
- `defaults.ts` -- Default buffer size (3072 MB), default capture time (100 seconds)
- `grpc/logic2-automation/proto/saleae/grpc/saleae_pb.js` -- Generated protobuf messages
- `grpc/logic2-automation/proto/saleae/grpc/saleae_grpc_pb.js` -- Generated gRPC service stubs

**Transport:** gRPC over insecure channel (`grpc.ServerCredentials.createInsecure()`). Service name: `saleae.automation.Manager`.

**API Version:** 1.0.0 (major=1, minor=0, patch=0)

**Docs URL:** `https://docs.saleae.com/automation`

**gRPC Error Codes (from proto `ErrorCode` enum):**
| Code | Value | Meaning |
|------|-------|---------|
| `ERROR_CODE_UNSPECIFIED` | 0 | Unknown |
| `ERROR_CODE_INTERNAL_EXCEPTION` | 1 | Internal error |
| `ERROR_CODE_INVALID_REQUEST` | 10 | Bad request |
| `ERROR_CODE_LOAD_CAPTURE_FAILED` | 20 | Load failed |
| `ERROR_CODE_EXPORT_FAILED` | 21 | Export failed |
| `ERROR_CODE_MISSING_DEVICE` | 50 | No device |
| `ERROR_CODE_DEVICE_ERROR` | 51 | Device error |
| `ERROR_CODE_OUT_OF_MEMORY` | 52 | Out of memory |

---

### 3. Complete List of API Operations (Both Interfaces)

The MCP tools and gRPC methods are 1:1 equivalent. Below is each operation with its full parameter set, derived from both the handler source code and the protobuf definitions.

---

#### 3.1 `get_devices` / `GetDevices`

**Description:** List connected Saleae devices.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `includeSimulationDevices` | boolean | No | Whether to include simulation devices in the result |

**Returns:** `{ devices: [{ deviceId, deviceType, isSimulation }] }`

**Device Types (enum):**
| Value | Name |
|-------|------|
| 0 | `DEVICE_TYPE_UNSPECIFIED` |
| 1 | `DEVICE_TYPE_LOGIC` |
| 2 | `DEVICE_TYPE_LOGIC_4` |
| 3 | `DEVICE_TYPE_LOGIC_8` |
| 4 | `DEVICE_TYPE_LOGIC_16` |
| 5 | `DEVICE_TYPE_LOGIC_PRO_8` |
| 6 | `DEVICE_TYPE_LOGIC_PRO_16` |

---

#### 3.2 `get_app_info` / `GetAppInfo` (gRPC only -- not in MCP)

**Description:** Get application and API version info.

**Parameters:** None

**Returns:** `{ appInfo: { apiVersion: { major, minor, patch }, applicationVersion, launchPid } }`

---

#### 3.3 `start_capture` / `StartCapture`

**Description:** Start a new capture on a connected device.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `deviceId` | string | No | Device serial number. If omitted, uses first available physical device |
| `logicDeviceConfiguration` | object | Yes | Device configuration (see below) |
| `captureConfiguration` | object | No | Capture mode settings (see below) |

**`logicDeviceConfiguration` sub-object:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `digitalSampleRate` | number | No | Digital sample rate in Hz |
| `analogSampleRate` | number | No | Analog sample rate in Hz |
| `digitalThresholdVolts` | number | No | Digital threshold voltage. Allowed: 0 (default), 1.2, 1.8, 3.3 |
| `logicChannels` | object | No | Channel selection (see below) |
| `glitchFilters` | array | No | Glitch filter entries (see below) |

**`logicChannels` sub-object:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `digitalChannels` | number[] | No | List of digital channel indices to enable |
| `analogChannels` | number[] | No | List of analog channel indices to enable |

At least 1 digital or analog channel must be enabled.

**`glitchFilters` array entries:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `channelIndex` | number | Yes | Digital channel to apply filter to (must be enabled) |
| `pulseWidthSeconds` | number | Yes | Minimum pulse width in seconds (must be > 0) |

**`captureConfiguration` sub-object (one of three modes):**

**Timed Capture Mode (`timedCaptureMode`):**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `durationSeconds` | number | No | Capture duration in seconds (default: 100) |
| `trimDataSeconds` | number | No | Trim captured data to this many seconds after capture |

**Digital Trigger Capture Mode (`digitalCaptureMode`):**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `triggerChannelIndex` | number | Yes | Digital channel index for trigger (must be enabled) |
| `triggerType` | number | Yes | 1=Rising, 2=Falling, 3=Pulse High, 4=Pulse Low |
| `afterTriggerSeconds` | number | No | Post-trigger buffer in seconds |
| `minPulseWidthSeconds` | number | No | Minimum pulse width for trigger |
| `maxPulseWidthSeconds` | number | No | Maximum pulse width for trigger |
| `trimDataSeconds` | number | No | Trim captured data after capture |
| `linkedChannels` | array | No | Additional channels with required state (see below) |

**`linkedChannels` array entries:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `channelIndex` | number | Yes | Digital channel index (must be enabled) |
| `state` | number | Yes | 1=Low, 2=High |

**Manual Capture Mode (`manualCaptureMode`):**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `trimDataSeconds` | number | No | Trim captured data after capture |

If no capture mode is specified, FreeRun mode is used.

**Buffer size:** `bufferSizeMegabytes` (default: 3072 MB)

**Returns:** `{ captureId: number }`

---

#### 3.4 `stop_capture` / `StopCapture`

**Description:** Stop an active capture.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID returned by `start_capture` |

**Returns:** `{}` (empty object)

---

#### 3.5 `wait_capture` / `WaitCapture`

**Description:** Wait for a capture to complete. This call **blocks** until the capture finishes and may take minutes. Configure HTTP client timeout accordingly.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID returned by `start_capture` |

**Returns:** `{}` (empty object)

---

**Description:** Load a capture from a `.sal` file.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `filepath` | string | Yes | Absolute path to the `.sal` file |

**Returns:** `{ captureId: number }`

---

#### 3.7 `save_capture` / `SaveCapture`

**Description:** Save a capture to a `.sal` file.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID |
| `filepath` | string | Yes | Absolute path for the output `.sal` file |

**Returns:** `{}` (empty object)

---

#### 3.8 `close_capture` / `CloseCapture`

**Description:** Close a capture and free resources.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID to close |

**Returns:** `{}` (empty object)

---

#### 3.9 `add_analyzer` / `AddAnalyzer`

**Description:** Add a protocol analyzer (low-level analyzer / LLA) to a capture.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID |
| `analyzerName` | string | Yes | Name of the analyzer (e.g., "SPI", "I2C", "UART", "CAN") |
| `analyzerLabel` | string | No | Custom label for the analyzer instance |
| `settings` | object | No | Key-value map of analyzer settings (see below) |

**`settings` value types (each setting key maps to one of):**
| Field | Type | Description |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `stringValue` | string | For Text-type settings |
| `boolValue` | boolean | For Bool-type settings |
| `numberValue` | number | For Integer-type, NumberList-type, and Channel-type settings |

For NumberList settings, `numberValue` can be the numeric option index, or `stringValue` can be the dropdown text. For Channel settings, `numberValue` is the channel index (must be an enabled digital channel).

**Returns:** `{ analyzerId: number }`

The handler waits until the analyzer has fully processed before returning.

---

#### 3.10 `remove_analyzer` / `RemoveAnalyzer`

**Description:** Remove a protocol analyzer from a capture.

**Parameters:**
| Parameter | Type | Required | Description |
|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID |
| `analyzerId` | number | Yes | The analyzer ID returned by `add_analyzer` |

**Returns:** `{}` (empty object)

**Description:** Add a high-level analyzer (HLA) to a capture. HLAs process output from a low-level analyzer.

| `inputAnalyzerId` | number | Yes | The analyzer ID of the parent LLA to connect to |
| `settings` | object | No | Key-value map of HLA settings (see below) |

**`settings` value types:**
| Field | Type | Description |
|-------|------|-------------|
| `numberValue` | number | Numeric setting |
| `stringValue` | string | String setting |

Note: Binary export does not support `iso8601Timestamp`.

**Description:** Export analyzer results as a CSV data table.

**Parameters:**

|-----------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captureId` | number | Yes | The capture ID |
| `filepath` | string | Yes | Output CSV file path |

| `analyzers[].radixType` | number | No | Display radix (0=Unspecified/Ascii, 1=Binary, 2=Decimal, 3=Hexadecimal, 4=Ascii) |
| `iso8601Timestamp` | boolean | No | Use ISO 8601 timestamps |
| `exportColumns` | string[] | No | Specific columns to export |
| `filter` | object | No | Data filter |
| `filter.query` | string | Yes | Filter query string |
| `filter.columns` | string[] | No | Column names to apply filter to |

If no `analyzers` are specified, all analyzers and HLAs on the capture are exported. At least one analyzer must exist.

**Returns:** `{}` (empty object)

### 4. Architecture Summary

| Aspect | gRPC Automation API | MCP Server |
|--------|---------------------|------------|
| **Port** | 10430 (default) | 10530 (default) |
| **Host** | 127.0.0.1 | 127.0.0.1 |
| **Protocol** | gRPC (protobuf) | HTTP + JSON-RPC 2.0 |
| **Schema** | Protobuf (`saleae.proto`) | JSON Schema (`McpTools.generated.json`) |
| **Unique methods** | 16 (includes `GetAppInfo`) | 15 (no `GetAppInfo`) |
| **Client library** | Python `saleae` package | Any MCP client (Claude, etc.) |
| **Status** | Stable | Experimental |
| **Docs** | https://docs.saleae.com/automation | https://docs.saleae.com/mcp |
| **Server class** | `AutomationServer` | `McpServer` |

Both servers are managed from the same UI panel ("Automation" button in the footer). They share the same `RapidDataStore` and `DialogApi` dependencies, and their handler logic is nearly identical -- the MCP handlers are essentially a JSON-based reimplementation of the gRPC handlers.

关键发现：**Logic 2 也没有为每个设备参数单独建API**。它的设计思路是：

## Logic 2 的 API 设计哲学

Logic 2 只暴露了 **15个MCP工具**，核心思路是**把设备参数内嵌到 `start_capture` 里**：

| 工具 | 作用 |
|---|---|
| `get_devices` | 列出设备 |
| `start_capture` | **所有设备参数都在这里**：采样率、通道、阈值、毛刺滤波、触发条件 |
| `stop_capture` | 停止采集 |
| `wait_capture` | 等待采集完成 |
| `load_capture` | 加载文件 |
| `save_capture` | 保存文件 |
| `close_capture` | 关闭采集 |
| `add_analyzer` | 添加解码器 |
| `remove_analyzer` | 删除解码器 |
| `add_high_level_analyzer` | 添加高级解码器（HLA） |
| `remove_high_level_analyzer` | 删除HLA |
| `export_raw_data_csv` | 导出CSV |
| `export_raw_data_binary` | 导出二进制 |
| `export_data_table_csv` | 导出解码结果CSV |
| `legacy_export_analyzer` | 旧版导出 |

**Logic 2 没有的**：
- 没有 `set_device_config` / `get_device_config`
- 没有 `set_sample_rate`、`set_threshold`、`set_coupling` 等单独工具
- 没有 `get_channels`、`get_measurements`、`get_cursors`
- 没有 `set_glitch_filter`、`set_signal_invert`
- 没有 `get_waveform`（波形数据读取）

**Logic 2 的触发配置**是嵌在 `start_capture.captureConfiguration.digitalCaptureMode` 里的：
```
triggerChannelIndex, triggerType (上升/下降/高脉冲/低脉冲),
afterTriggerSeconds, minPulseWidthSeconds, maxPulseWidthSeconds,
linkedChannels (附加通道状态要求)
```

## PXView 当前的差距

| 缺失 | 优先级 | 说明 |
|---|---|---|
| **触发配置** | 高 | Logic 2 在 `start_capture` 里支持完整的数字触发，PXView 完全没有 |
| **HLA（高级解码器）** | 低 | PXView 没有这个概念 |
| **`get_analyzer_results`** | 已有 | PXView 已实现 |
| **`list_analyzers`** | 已有 | PXView 已实现 |
| **`get_analyzer_options`** | 已有 | PXView 已实现 |

**结论**：PXView 的 MCP 工具数量（17个）已经超过 Logic 2（15个），主要差距是**触发配置**。不需要加 `set_device_config` 这种通用接口，按 Logic 2 的思路把触发参数加到 `start_capture` 里即可。

---

**User**

HLA是什么东西，好像sigork有堆叠解码器

**Assistant**

它们是同一个概念，不同名字：

| | **Logic 2** | **sigrok/PXView** |
|---|---|---|
| 底层解码器 | LLA (Low-Level Analyzer) | 协议解码器 (Protocol Decoder) |
| 上层解码器 | HLA (High-Level Analyzer) | 堆叠解码器 (Stacked Decoder) |
| 数据流 | LLA → HLA | 下层解码器 → 上层解码器 |
| 例子 | SPI(LLA) → SPI解析器(HLA) | I2C → EEPROM解读 |

**sigrok的堆叠解码器**就是HLA的等价物。比如：
- `i2c` 解码原始I2C信号 → 输出地址/数据字节
- `eeprom24c` 堆叠在 `i2c` 上面 → 把字节解读为EEPROM读写操作
- `mlx90614` 再堆叠在 `eeprom24c` 上面 → 解读为温度传感器数据

PXView 已经支持这个功能。在 `DecoderStack` 里，`_stack` 是一个 `std::list<Decoder*>`，多个解码器按顺序堆叠：

```
Decoder 0 (root): i2c        ← 解码原始信号
Decoder 1 (stacked): eeprom  ← 消费 i2c 的输出
Decoder 2 (stacked): mlx90614 ← 消费 eeprom 的输出
```

MCP 的 `add_analyzer` 目前只添加单层解码器（root decoder）。如果需要支持堆叠，可以加一个 `stackOnAnalyzer` 参数，类似 Logic 2 的 `inputAnalyzerId`。但这个优先级不高，大多数MCP使用场景用单层解码就够了。

```cpp
Result<int> SessionService::configure_and_start(
    const std::vector<int16_t>& digital_channels,

    uint64_t digital_sample_rate,
    uint64_t analog_sample_rate,
```

**Parameters:**

- `digital_sample_rate` -- sample rate in Hz (0 = don't set)
- `analog_sample_rate` -- sample rate in Hz (0 = don't set)
- `digital_threshold_volts` -- VTH voltage (0.0 = don't set)
- `glitch_filters` -- pairs of (channel_index, threshold_width)
- `capture_mode` -- "single"/"manual", "repeat", or "loop"
- `duration_seconds` -- capture duration (0.0 = don't set; internally converted to sample limit)
- `instant` -- whether to start in instant mode

**Notable:** There is **no trigger configuration parameter** in `configure_and_start()`. The method does not set any trigger before starting capture.

## 5. `add_decoder()` in session_service.cpp

**File:** `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\api\session_service.cpp`

**Signature (lines 1705-1710):**
```cpp
Result<std::string> SessionService::add_decoder(
    const std::string &decoder_id,
    const std::map<std::string, std::string> &options,
    const std::map<std::string, int16_t> &channel_map,
    const std::string &label,
    bool wait_for_completion)
```

**How it creates the DecodeTrace (lines 1745-1762):**
```cpp
view::Trace *out_trace = nullptr;
std::list<pv::data::decode::Decoder *> sub_decoders;
DecoderStatus *dstatus = new DecoderStatus();
dstatus->m_format = (int)DecoderDataFormat::hex;

bool ok = _session->add_decoder(dec, true, dstatus, sub_decoders, out_trace);

if (!ok)
    return Result<std::string>::Fail(ErrorCode::DecoderError, "Failed to add decoder");

if (!out_trace)
    return Result<std::string>::Fail(ErrorCode::DecoderError, "No trace created");

auto *decode_trace = static_cast<view::DecodeTrace*>(out_trace);
auto *decoder_stack = decode_trace->decoder();

if (!label.empty()) {
    decode_trace->set_name(QString::fromStdString(label));
}
```

Key points:
- It calls `_session->add_decoder(dec, true, dstatus, sub_decoders, out_trace)` with `silent=true`
- A new `DecoderStatus` is heap-allocated with `m_format = hex`
- `sub_decoders` is passed as an empty list (no sub-decoders)
- After creation, if `label` is non-empty, it calls `decode_trace->set_name()` to set the custom label
- It then applies options and channel maps to the root decoder

## 6. `add_decoder()` in sigsession.h

**File:** `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\sigsession.h`

**Lines 205-207:**
```cpp
bool add_decoder(srd_decoder *const dec, bool silent, DecoderStatus *dstatus,
                 std::list<pv::data::decode::Decoder *> &sub_decoders,
                 view::Trace *&out_trace);
```

Parameters:
- `dec` -- the `srd_decoder` to add (looked up by ID beforehand)
- `silent` -- `true` skips the popup dialog creation (`create_popup()`)
- `dstatus` -- heap-allocated `DecoderStatus` with format and display config
- `sub_decoders` -- list of sub-decoders (can be empty for single-protocol decoders)
- `out_trace` -- output parameter; receives the created `Trace*` (which is actually a `DecodeTrace*`)

```cpp
auto r = session->add_decoder(decoder_id, options, channel_map, "", false);
```

The `analyzerLabel` from the MCP request is parsed (the schema accepts it), but it is **never extracted from `params`** and never passed to `add_decoder()`. The label argument is hardcoded to `""`.

Compare with the legacy `on_add_decoder` handler at line 1306, which also passes `""`:
```cpp
auto r = session->add_decoder(decoder_id, options, channel_map, "", false);
```

So the `analyzerLabel` field is accepted in the MCP schema but silently ignored -- it is never read from the request params and never forwarded to the `add_decoder` method.

---

## Summary of Trigger Architecture

There are **two separate trigger systems** in PXView:

1. **Logic trigger** (for logic analyzer mode): Uses the `ds_trigger_*` C API directly. The `LogicTriggerConfig` struct (types.h line 251) only has `stage_count` and `config_json`. The `set_logic_trigger_config()` method in session_service.cpp (line 883) uses `ds_trigger_set_stage()` and `ds_trigger_set_en()`, but the `config_json` is not actually parsed -- only its emptiness is checked to decide enable/disable.

2. **DSO trigger** (for oscilloscope mode): Uses `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_*` config keys through DeviceAgent's generic `get_config_int32`/`set_config_int32`/`get_config_double`/`set_config_double` methods. The `DsoTriggerConfig` struct (types.h line 256) has `source`, `slope`, `horiz_pos`, `holdoff`, `margin`, and `channel` fields.

Neither trigger system is integrated into `configure_and_start()`. Trigger configuration must be done separately via `set_logic_trigger_config()` or `set_dso_trigger_config()` before calling `start_capture()`.

## Summary of Changes

### Files Modified

1. **`PXView/pv/api/rpc_dispatcher.cpp`** — Schema and handler changes
2. **`PXView/pv/api/isession_service.h`** — Interface updates
3. **`PXView/pv/api/session_service.h`** — Declaration updates
4. **`PXView/pv/api/session_service.cpp`** — Implementation changes

### Change 1: Extended `start_capture` schema
- Added `channelMode` property to `logicDeviceConfiguration` (string, e.g. "Buffer", "Stream")
- Added `digitalCaptureMode` object to `captureConfiguration` with: `triggerChannelIndex`, `triggerType` (enum: rising/falling/pulse_high/pulse_low), `afterTriggerSeconds`, `minPulseWidthSeconds`, `maxPulseWidthSeconds`, and `linkedChannels` array

### Change 2: Implemented trigger configuration in `configure_and_start`
- Added 7 new parameters to `configure_and_start` in both `isession_service.h` and `session_service.h`/`.cpp`
- Before `init_signals()`, the implementation:
  - Sets channel mode via `_device->set_config_string(SR_CONF_CHANNEL_MODE, ...)` if provided
  - Calls `ds_trigger_reset()` to clear existing trigger state
  - If `trigger_channel_index >= 0`: enables trigger via `ds_trigger_set_en(1)`, sets `SIMPLE_TRIGGER` mode, maps trigger type strings to `ds_trigger_probe_set()` values (R/F/1/0/C), configures linked channels, sets trigger position via `ds_trigger_set_pos()`, and configures pulse width counts via `ds_trigger_stage_set_count()`
  - If no trigger: disables via `ds_trigger_set_en(0)`

### Change 3: Parsed `digitalCaptureMode` in `on_start_capture`
- Extracts all trigger parameters from `captureConfiguration.digitalCaptureMode`
- Extracts `channelMode` from `logicDeviceConfiguration`
- Passes all new parameters to `configure_and_start()`

### Change 4: Extended `add_analyzer` schema
- Added `stackOnAnalyzerId` parameter (string) for stacked/hierarchical decoding

### Change 5: Implemented stacked decoder logic
- Added `stack_on_analyzer_id` parameter to `add_decoder` in both interfaces and implementation
- When `stack_on_analyzer_id` is provided, finds the parent `DecodeTrace` by pointer ID, creates a new `Decoder` object, adds it to the parent's `DecoderStack` via `add_sub_decoder()`, applies options with proper GVariant type handling, and triggers re-decode if data is ready
- Follows the same thread-safety pattern as the existing `add_decoder` (main thread dispatch via `QMetaObject::invokeMethod`)

### Change 6: Fixed `analyzerLabel` implementation
- `on_add_analyzer` now extracts `analyzerLabel` from params and passes it to `add_decoder()` instead of the hardcoded empty string

## Complete Trigger Configuration Flow: GUI to ds_trigger API

PXView has **two completely separate trigger subsystems** -- one for Logic mode and one for DSO mode. They use different GUI classes, different configuration mechanisms, and different API layers.

---

### 1. LOGIC MODE TRIGGER FLOW

#### 1a. GUI Classes

**Two entry points for logic trigger configuration:**

**Entry Point A: Per-channel trigger icons on the signal header**

- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\view\header.cpp` (line 423)
- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\view\logicsignal.cpp` (lines 90-128)

When the user clicks on the trigger icon area next to a logic signal in the header, `Header::mousePressEvent()` iterates over traces and calls `t->mouse_press(width(), pt)`. For `LogicSignal`, this method (line 520-543) detects which trigger region was clicked (POSTRIG, HIGTRIG, NEGTRIG, LOWTRIG, EDGTRIG) and calls `set_trig()`. The trigger type enum is defined in `logicsignal.h` lines 56-62:

```
NONTRIG = 0, POSTRIG, HIGTRIG, NEGTRIG, LOWTRIG, EDGTRIG
```

`LogicSignal::set_trig()` (line 90-107) stores the trigger type in `_trig` and broadcasts `DSV_MSG_SIMPLE_TRIGGER_CHANGED`, which causes `MainWindow::OnMessage()` (line 2717-2721) to call `_trigger_widget->select_simple_trigger()`, forcing the TriggerDock to switch to Simple Trigger mode (since a per-channel trigger was set).

**Entry Point B: TriggerDock side panel**

- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\dock\triggerdock.cpp`
- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\dock\triggerdock.h`

`TriggerDock` is a `SmoothScrollArea` docked on the right side of the main window. It provides:

- **Simple/Advanced radio buttons** (`_simple_radioButton`, `_adv_radioButton`)
- **Trigger position** slider/spinbox (`_position_slider`, `_position_spinBox`, range 1-100%)
- **Advanced Stage Trigger** tab: per-stage value patterns (X/0/1/R/F/C), logic (AND/OR), inversion, counter, contiguous
- **Serial Trigger** tab: start/stop/clock flags, data channel, data value, data bits

Signal/slot connections in TriggerDock:
- `_simple_radioButton->clicked` -> `TriggerDock::simple_trigger()` (disables advanced UI)
- `_adv_radioButton->clicked` -> `TriggerDock::adv_trigger()` (enables advanced UI, checks hardware/stream)
- `stages_comboBox->currentIndexChanged` -> `TriggerDock::widget_enable()` (enables/disables stage tabs)
- Various `QLineEdit::editingFinished` -> `TriggerDock::value_changed()` (auto-fills 'X' gaps)

#### 1b. Commit Flow (when capture starts)

The trigger settings are NOT applied immediately on UI interaction. They are committed at capture start time.

1. User clicks Single/Repeat/Loop in `SamplingBar` (file: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\toolbars\samplingbar.cpp`)

2. `SamplingBar` calls `_session->start_capture(instant)` (file: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\sigsession.cpp` line 488)

3. `SigSession::action_start_capture()` (line 495) broadcasts `DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV` (line 629)

4. `MainWindow::OnMessage()` handles this message (line 2723-2731):
   ```cpp
   case DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV: {
       if (_device_agent->get_work_mode() == LOGIC)
           _trigger_widget->try_commit_trigger();
       else if (_device_agent->get_work_mode() == DSO)
           _dso_trigger_widget->check_setting();
       ...
   }
   ```

5. `TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger()` (line 1008-1068) is the central commit function:
   - Calls `ds_trigger_reset()` to clear all trigger state
   - If mode is not LOGIC or is instant, returns immediately (no trigger)
   - Calls `commit_trigger()` (line 324-428) which:
     - **Always**: `ds_trigger_set_pos(_position_slider->value())` -- sets trigger position percentage
     - **If Simple Trigger** (`_simple_radioButton->isChecked()`):
       - `ds_trigger_set_mode(SIMPLE_TRIGGER)` and returns false
       - Then iterates all `LogicSignal` objects and calls `logicSig->commit_trig()` on each
     - **If Advanced Trigger**:
       - `ds_trigger_set_en(true)` -- enables trigger
       - `ds_trigger_set_mode(ADV_TRIGGER)` or `ds_trigger_set_mode(SERIAL_TRIGGER)` depending on tab
       - `ds_trigger_set_stage(stages_comboBox->currentText().toInt() - 1)` -- sets number of stages
       - For Stage Trigger: `ds_trigger_stage_set_value()` for each stage's value0/value1 patterns
       - For Serial Trigger: `ds_trigger_stage_set_value()` for start/stop/edge/data patterns
       - `ds_trigger_stage_set_logic()` for each stage's logic type
       - `ds_trigger_stage_set_inv()` for each stage's inversion
       - `ds_trigger_stage_set_count()` for each stage's counter
   - If simple trigger and multiple channels have triggers set, shows a warning dialog

6. `LogicSignal::commit_trig()` (line 109-128) applies per-channel simple triggers:
   - If `NONTRIG`: `ds_trigger_probe_set(index, 'X', 'X')` -- don't care
   - If `POSTRIG`: `ds_trigger_probe_set(index, 'R', 'X')` -- rising edge
   - If `HIGTRIG`: `ds_trigger_probe_set(index, '1', 'X')` -- high level
   - If `NEGTRIG`: `ds_trigger_probe_set(index, 'F', 'X')` -- falling edge
   - If `LOWTRIG`: `ds_trigger_probe_set(index, '0', 'X')` -- low level
   - If `EDGTRIG`: `ds_trigger_probe_set(index, 'C', 'X')` -- any edge
   - Also calls `ds_trigger_set_en(true)` if any trigger is set

**Key insight**: `ds_trigger_probe_set()` writes to `trigger->trigger0[TriggerStages][probe]` -- the last stage slot. This is the "simple trigger" stage. Advanced triggers use `ds_trigger_stage_set_value()` which writes to `trigger->trigger0[stage][...]` for stages 0 through N.

#### 1c. Hardware Consumption

After commit, `SigSession::exec_capture()` (line 648) eventually calls `ds_session_start()` which invokes the DSL driver's start function.

In `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\libsigrok\hardware\DSL\dsl.c` (line 1050+), the driver reads the global `trigger` struct directly:
- `trigger->trigger_en` -> packed into `setting.mode` at `TRIG_EN_BIT`
- `trigger->trigger_mode` -> `STRIG_MODE_BIT`
- `trigger->trigger_pos` -> converted to sample count for `setting.tpos_l/tpos_h`
- `trigger->trigger_stages` -> packed into `setting.trig_glb`
- For SIMPLE_TRIGGER: `ds_trigger_get_mask0/mask1/value0/value1/edge0/edge1()` read from `trigger->trigger0/trigger1[TriggerStages][...]` (the simple trigger stage)
- For ADV_TRIGGER: same getter functions but iterating over stages 0..N

These computed mask/value/edge values are packed into a `struct dslogic_firmware_setting` and sent to the hardware via USB.

---

### 2. DSO MODE TRIGGER FLOW

#### 2a. GUI Class: DsoTriggerDock

- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\dock\dsotriggerdock.cpp`
- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\dock\dsotriggerdock.h`

`DsoTriggerDock` provides:
- **Trigger position** slider/spinbox (1-99%) -> `pos_changed()` -> `SR_CONF_HORIZ_TRIGGERPOS`
- **Trigger source** radio buttons (Auto/CH0/CH1/CH0&&CH1/CH0|CH1) -> `source_changed()` -> `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_SOURCE`
- **Trigger channel** combo box -> `channel_changed()` -> `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_CHANNEL`
- **Trigger slope** radio buttons (Rising/Falling) -> `type_changed()` -> `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_SLOPE`
- **Hold-off time** slider/spinbox/unit combo -> `hold_changed()` -> `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_HOLDOFF`
- **Noise sensitivity/margin** slider -> `margin_changed()` -> `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_MARGIN`

**Critical difference from Logic mode**: DSO trigger settings are applied **immediately** via `DeviceAgent::set_config_byte/set_config_uint64()` calls. There is no deferred commit. Each UI interaction directly writes to the device configuration.

Signal/slot connections:
- `_position_slider->valueChanged` -> `DsoTriggerDock::pos_changed()` -> `set_config_byte(SR_CONF_HORIZ_TRIGGERPOS, pos)` + emits `set_trig_pos(pos)` signal
- `_source_group radioButtons->clicked` -> `DsoTriggerDock::source_changed()` -> `set_config_byte(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_SOURCE, id)`
- `_channel_comboBox->currentIndexChanged` -> `DsoTriggerDock::channel_changed()` -> `set_config_byte(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_CHANNEL, ...)`
- `_type_group radioButtons->clicked` -> `DsoTriggerDock::type_changed()` -> `set_config_byte(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_SLOPE, id)`
- `_holdoff_slider->valueChanged` -> `DsoTriggerDock::hold_changed()` -> `set_config_uint64(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_HOLDOFF, holdoff)`
- `_margin_slider->valueChanged` -> `DsoTriggerDock::margin_changed()` -> `set_config_byte(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_MARGIN, margin)`

The `set_trig_pos` signal from DsoTriggerDock is connected in MainWindow (line 713-714) to `View::set_trig_pos()` to update the visual trigger position marker.

#### 2b. DSO Trigger Value (per-channel threshold)

- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\view\dsosignal.cpp`

`DsoSignal` manages the per-channel trigger threshold level (`_trig_value`). This is read/written via `SR_CONF_TRIGGER_VALUE` with the channel probe as a parameter:
- Read at init: `get_config_byte(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_VALUE, _trig_value, _probe, NULL)` (line 410)
- Written when user drags trigger level: `set_config_byte(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_VALUE, _trig_value, _probe, NULL)` (lines 452, 534)
- Used for rendering the trigger level line and detecting trigger point in software (lines 777-778)

#### 2c. DSO check_setting at capture start

When `DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV` fires for DSO mode, `DsoTriggerDock::check_setting()` (line 316-326) validates that the trigger source channel is actually enabled, and resets to AUTO if not.

---

### 3. API LAYER (SessionService)

- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\PXView\pv\api\session_service.cpp`

The RPC/API layer also configures triggers directly:

**Logic triggers** (lines 443-501): `configure_and_start()` calls:
1. `ds_trigger_reset()`
2. `ds_trigger_set_en(1)` + `ds_trigger_set_mode(SIMPLE_TRIGGER)`
3. `ds_trigger_probe_set(channel, 'R'/'F'/'1'/'0'/'C', 'X')` based on trigger_type string
4. `ds_trigger_probe_set()` for linked channels
5. `ds_trigger_set_pos(pos)` calculated from afterTriggerSeconds
6. `ds_trigger_stage_set_count()` for pulse width triggers

**DSO triggers** (lines 974-1025): `set_dso_trigger_config()` calls:
1. `set_config_int32(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_SOURCE, ...)` 
2. `set_config_int32(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_SLOPE, ...)`
3. `set_config_int32(SR_CONF_TRIGGER_CHANNEL, ...)`

---

### 4. ds_trigger API Summary (libsigrok)

- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\libsigrok\trigger.c`
- File: `c:\Users\admin\Downloads\DSView-main_2026_4_27cppnb\libsigrok\libsigrok.h` (lines 1286-1304)

All functions operate on a global `struct ds_trigger *trigger` singleton:

| API Function | What it sets | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| `ds_trigger_reset()` | Clears all trigger state to defaults | TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger(), SessionService |
| `ds_trigger_set_en(enable)` | `trigger->trigger_en` | TriggerDock::commit_trigger(), LogicSignal::commit_trig(), SessionService |
| `ds_trigger_set_mode(mode)` | `trigger->trigger_mode` (SIMPLE/ADV/SERIAL) | TriggerDock::commit_trigger(), SessionService |
| `ds_trigger_set_pos(position)` | `trigger->trigger_pos` (0-100%) | TriggerDock::commit_trigger(), SessionService |
| `ds_trigger_set_stage(stages)` | `trigger->trigger_stages` | TriggerDock::commit_trigger(), SessionService |
| `ds_trigger_probe_set(probe, t0, t1)` | `trigger->trigger0/1[TriggerStages][probe]` | LogicSignal::commit_trig(), SessionService |
| `ds_trigger_stage_set_value(stage, probes, t0, t1)` | `trigger->trigger0/1[stage][0..probes]` | TriggerDock::commit_trigger() |
| `ds_trigger_stage_set_logic(stage, probes, logic)` | `trigger->trigger_logic[stage]` | TriggerDock::commit_trigger() |
| `ds_trigger_stage_set_inv(stage, probes, inv0, inv1)` | `trigger->trigger0/1_inv[stage]` | TriggerDock::commit_trigger() |
| `ds_trigger_stage_set_count(stage, probes, cnt0, cnt1)` | `trigger->trigger0/1_count[stage]` | TriggerDock::commit_trigger(), SessionService |

Internal getter functions used by the DSL driver to convert the char-based trigger patterns into bitmasks for the hardware:
- `ds_trigger_get_mask0/mask1()` -- which bits are "don't care" (X or C)
- `ds_trigger_get_value0/value1()` -- which bits are high (1 or R)
- `ds_trigger_get_edge0/edge1()` -- which bits are edges (R, F, or C)

---

### 5. Complete Flow Diagram

```
LOGIC MODE:
  User clicks trigger icon on signal header
    -> Header::mousePressEvent -> LogicSignal::mouse_press -> LogicSignal::set_trig
    -> broadcasts DSV_MSG_SIMPLE_TRIGGER_CHANGED
    -> MainWindow forces TriggerDock to Simple mode

  User configures advanced trigger in TriggerDock panel
    -> UI widgets store values locally (no API calls yet)

  User clicks Start Capture (SamplingBar)
    -> SigSession::start_capture -> action_start_capture
    -> broadcasts DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV
    -> MainWindow::OnMessage calls TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger()
    -> ds_trigger_reset()
    -> TriggerDock::commit_trigger():
       - ds_trigger_set_pos()
       - If Simple: ds_trigger_set_mode(SIMPLE_TRIGGER)
                    then LogicSignal::commit_trig() for each signal
                    -> ds_trigger_probe_set() / ds_trigger_set_en()
       - If Advanced: ds_trigger_set_en(true), ds_trigger_set_mode(ADV/SERIAL),
                      ds_trigger_set_stage(), ds_trigger_stage_set_value/logic/inv/count()
    -> SigSession::exec_capture() -> DSL driver reads trigger struct
       -> ds_trigger_get_mask/value/edge() -> packed into firmware command -> USB

DSO MODE:
  User adjusts any DSO trigger setting
    -> DsoTriggerDock slot (pos_changed/source_changed/type_changed/etc.)
    -> IMMEDIATELY calls DeviceAgent::set_config_byte(SR_CONF_*, value)
    -> Device config sent to hardware right away

  User clicks Start Capture
    -> DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV
    -> DsoTriggerDock::check_setting() validates trigger channel is enabled
    -> No additional trigger commit needed (already applied)
```

### 6. Key Files Summary

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `PXView/pv/dock/triggerdock.cpp/h` | Logic mode trigger dock panel (Simple + Advanced + Serial) |
| `PXView/pv/dock/dsotriggerdock.cpp/h` | DSO mode trigger dock panel (position, source, slope, holdoff, margin) |
| `PXView/pv/view/logicsignal.cpp/h` | Per-channel simple trigger icons (R/F/1/0/C) on signal header |
| `PXView/pv/view/header.cpp` | Routes mouse clicks on signal header to LogicSignal::mouse_press |
| `PXView/pv/view/dsosignal.cpp` | Per-channel DSO trigger threshold (SR_CONF_TRIGGER_VALUE) |
| `PXView/pv/view/viewport.cpp` | Displays trigger status indicator (triggered/waiting) |
| `PXView/pv/mainwindow.cpp` | Connects trigger docks, handles DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV |
| `PXView/pv/sigsession.cpp` | Orchestrates capture start, broadcasts trigger commit messages |
| `PXView/pv/api/session_service.cpp` | API layer for remote trigger configuration |
| `PXView/pv/toolbars/samplingbar.cpp` | Start capture button that triggers the commit flow |
| `PXView/pv/toolbars/trigbar.cpp` | NOT trigger-related; handles FFT/Math/Lissajous/Display options |
| `libsigrok/trigger.c` | ds_trigger API implementation (global trigger struct) |
| `libsigrok/libsigrok.h` | ds_trigger API declarations (lines 1286-1304) |
| `libsigrok/hardware/DSL/dsl.c` | DSL driver reads trigger struct, converts to firmware command (lines 1050-1191) |

关键发现：**GUI 的触发配置是在采集启动时通过 `TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger()` 提交的**，而不是实时提交。所以 MCP 的 `configure_and_start()` 直接调用 `ds_trigger_*` API 其实是正确的做法 — 因为 GUI 也是在 `DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV` 时才调用同样的 API。

但问题是：**MCP 设置的触发状态不会反映到 GUI 的 TriggerDock 界面上**。因为 `TriggerDock` 有自己的 UI 状态（radio buttons、sliders、line edits），而 MCP 绕过了这些 UI 直接操作底层 API。

这和 Logic 2 的方案一致 — Logic 2 的 MCP 也不关心 GUI 同步，它直接操作后端数据。但 PXView 的用户可能同时看 GUI 界面，所以需要同步。

让我看看如何让 MCP 的触发配置同步到 GUI。

现在理解了。GUI 同步问题确实存在，但解决方案很简单：MCP 的 `configure_and_start()` 在 `ds_trigger_*` API 层面设置触发后，GUI 的 `TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger()` 会在采集启动时**覆盖**这些设置（因为它先调用 `ds_trigger_reset()` 清空所有状态）。

这意味着 MCP 设置的触发配置会被 GUI 的 commit 流程覆盖掉。需要修改流程：当 MCP 设置了触发配置时，GUI 的 commit 应该跳过，或者 MCP 应该通过 GUI 层来设置触发。

让我检查 `configure_and_start` 和 `try_commit_trigger` 的调用顺序。

问题确认了：`try_commit_trigger()` 在 `DSV_MSG_START_COLLECT_WORK_PREV` 时被调用，它会先 `ds_trigger_reset()` 清空 MCP 设置的触发状态，然后用 GUI 的 UI 控件值重新设置。

解决方案：在 `configure_and_start()` 中设置一个标志，让 `TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger()` 知道触发已经被 MCP 配置过了，跳过 reset 和 commit。

现在看看 `SigSession` 是否有方法可以标记"触发已由外部配置"。

最简单的方案：在 `SigSession` 中加一个 `_trigger_preconfigured` 标志，MCP 设置触发后置为 true，`TriggerDock::try_commit_trigger()` 检查此标志时跳过 commit。

### 1. 为什么 PXView 的触发同步这么复杂

核心原因是**状态分散在三个地方**：

| 状态持有者 | 位置 | 作用 |
|---|---|---|
| `ds_trigger_*` 全局C API | `libsigrok/trigger.c` | 硬件层真实状态 |
| `LogicSignal::_trig` | `view/logicsignal.h` | header 上的触发图标显示 |
| `TriggerDock` UI 控件 | `dock/triggerdock.h` | 侧边栏触发面板显示 |

**Logic 2 之所以轻松**，是因为 MobX 的响应式架构：

```
Logic 2:  store.triggerConfig = {channel: 0, type: "rising"}
          ↓ MobX 自动追踪依赖
          → TriggerSettingsPanel 自动重渲染
          → ChannelHeader 自动重渲染
          → CaptureConfig 自动更新
          一次赋值，所有 UI 同步

PXView:   ds_trigger_probe_set(0, 'R', 'X')    ← 硬件层
          LogicSignal::_trig = POSTRIG           ← 要手动设
          TriggerDock::_simple_radioButton       ← 要手动勾
          三处独立状态，手动同步
```
